Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
. As
P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
, it follows that

P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
, and thus
a\in X_{\rm L}
.
The sixth rule of Table
3
yields
\sigma_{\rm L}\in FT^{*}(P)
.
Since
X_{\rm L}
is system-ended in
\sigma_{\rm L}

and
X
is system-ended in
\sigma
, Condition (
5
) holds as well.

Proposition A.3
.

abstraction explicit

\sigma\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))~~\Leftrightarrow~~\exists\rho\in FT^{*}(P).~\tau_{I}(\rho)=\sigma\cup I
.

Proof A.4
.

Since each state
R
reachable from
\tau_{I}(P)
satisfies
R{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in I
,
a trivial induction shows that

\sigma\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))\Leftrightarrow\sigma\cup I\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P)).

“
\Leftarrow
”: Let
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
and
\rho
survives abstraction from
I
.
By Condition (i) in the definition of abstraction survival,

\tau_{I}(\rho)
is of the form
\sigma\cup I
. In view of (*), it suffices to show that

\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
. I do so with structural induction on the
derivation of
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
. The case
\rho=\top
is trivial.

Assume
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the third rule of Table
3
.
Then
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
\rho\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
.
By induction
\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}))
.
By Table
1

\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
.
Hence
\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
by the third rule of Table
3
.

Assume
\rho=a\eta\in FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the second rule of Table
3
.
Then
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
\eta\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
.
By induction
\tau_{I}(\eta)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}))
.
By Table
1

\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
, with
\alpha=\tau
if
a\in I
and
\alpha=a
otherwise.
In the first case
\tau_{I}(\rho)=\tau_{I}(\eta)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
by the third rule of Table
3
.
In the second case
\tau_{I}(\rho)=a\tau_{I}(\eta)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
by the second rule of Table
3
.

Assume
\rho\mathbin{=}X\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the fourth rule of Table
3
.
Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
and
\eta\in FT^{*}(P)
.
If
\eta
has the form
a\zeta
, then
a\notin X
by .
In particular,
a\notin I
, using Condition (i) in the definition of abstraction survival.
Consequently,
\tau_{I}(\rho)=X\tau_{I}(\eta)
, for any possibly form of
\eta
.
Furthermore,
\tau_{I}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
.
Here I use that
I\subseteq X
.
By induction
\tau_{I}(\eta)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.
Hence
\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
by the fourth rule of Table
3
.

Assume
\rho\mathbin{=}X\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the fifth rule of Table
3
.
Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
,

P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
\rho\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
.
So
\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
and
\tau_{I}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all

\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
, using that
I\subseteq X
.
By induction
\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}))
.
Note that
\tau_{I}(\rho)
has the form
X\zeta
, although not necessarily with
\zeta=\tau_{I}(\eta)
.
The fifth rule of Table
3
yields
\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.

Assume
\rho\mathbin{=}X\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the sixth rule of Table
3
.
Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
,

P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
\eta\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
.
So
\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
and
\tau_{I}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
.
By induction
\tau_{I}(\eta)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}))
.
Using that
\rho
survives abstraction, I consider three alternatives for
\eta
.

•

Let
\eta=c_{0}\cdots c_{n}\top
with the
c_{i}\in I
.
Then
\tau_{I}(\rho)=X\top
.
The first and fourth rules of Table
3
yield
\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.

•

Let
\eta=c_{0}\cdots c_{n}X\zeta
with the
c_{i}\in I
.
Then
\tau_{I}(\rho)=\tau_{I}(\eta)=\tau_{I}(X\zeta)
, and
\tau_{I}(\eta)
has the form
X\zeta^{\prime}
.
The fifth rule of Table
3
yields
\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.

•

Let
\eta=c_{1}\cdots c_{n}a\zeta
with the
c_{i}\mathbin{\in}I
and
a\mathbin{\notin}I
.
Then
a\mathbin{\in}X
(using Condition (iii) of abstraction survival if
n>0
, and the side
condition of the sixth rule of Table
3
if
n=0
)
and
\tau_{I}(\rho)\mathbin{=}X\tau_{I}(\eta)
with
\tau_{I}(\eta)\mathbin{=}a\tau_{I}(\zeta)
.
The sixth rule of Table
3
yields
\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.

“
\Rightarrow
”: Let
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
; by (*) I may assume that
\sigma=\sigma\cup I
.
I have to find a
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
such that
\tau_{I}(\rho)=\sigma
.
I do so with structural induction on the derivation of
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.

Assume
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
is obtained from the first rule of Table
3
.
Take
\rho=\top
, so that
\tau_{I}(\rho)=\top=\sigma
.
The first rule of Table
3
yields
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
.

Assume
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
is obtained from the second rule of Table
3
.
Then
\sigma\mathbin{=}a\zeta
,
\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
and
\zeta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}))
.
So
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
a\mathbin{\notin}I
.
By induction, there is a
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
such that
\tau_{I}(\rho)\mathbin{=}\zeta
.
The second rule of Table
3
yields
a\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
.
Moreover,
\tau_{I}(a\rho)=a\zeta=\sigma
.

Assume
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
is obtained from the third rule of Table
3
.
Then
\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
and
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}))
.
So
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
with
\alpha\in I\cup\{\tau\}
.
By induction, there is a
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
such that
\tau_{I}(\rho)=\sigma
.
In case
\alpha=\tau
, the third rule of Table
3
yields
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
.
In case
\alpha\neq\tau
, the second rule of Table
3
yields
\alpha\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
;
moreover,
\tau_{I}(\alpha\rho)=\tau_{I}(\rho)=\sigma
.

Assume
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
is obtained from the fourth rule of Table
3
.
Then
\sigma\mathbin{=}X\zeta
,
\zeta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
and

\tau_{I}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
.
So
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
.
By induction, there is a
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
such that
\tau_{I}(\rho)\mathbin{=}\zeta
.
Hence
X\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
by the fourth rule of Table
3
.
In case
\rho
has the form
\top
or
Y\eta
, one has
\tau_{I}(X\rho)=X\tau_{I}(\rho)=X\zeta=\sigma
,
which needed to be shown.
So suppose
\rho
has the form
a\eta
. Then
a\notin X
by .
In particular,
a\notin I
, using Condition (i) in the definition of abstraction survival.
Consequently,
\tau_{I}(X\rho)=X\tau_{I}(\rho)=X\zeta=\sigma
.

Assume
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
is obtained from the fifth rule of Table
3
.
Then
\sigma\mathbin{=}X\zeta
,
\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
,
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}))
and

\tau_{I}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
.
So
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
.
By induction, there is a
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
such that
\tau_{I}(\rho)\mathbin{=}\sigma
.
This
\rho
must have the form
X\eta
.
Hence
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
by the fifth rule of Table
3
.

Finally, assume
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
is obtained from the sixth rule of Table
3
.
Then
\sigma\mathbin{=}Xa\zeta
,
a\in X
,
\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
,
a\zeta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
and

\tau_{I}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
.
So
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
.
By induction, there is a
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
such that
\tau_{I}(\rho)=a\zeta
.
So
a\notin I
and
\rho
has the form
c_{1}\dots c_{n}a\eta
with
c_{i}\in I\subseteq X

for
i=1,\dots,n
and
\tau_{I}(\eta)=\zeta
.
The sixth rule of Table
3
yields
X\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
.
Moreover,
\tau_{I}(X\rho)=Xa\tau_{I}(\eta)=Xa\zeta=\sigma
.

Proposition A.5
.

renaming explicit

\sigma\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))~~\Leftrightarrow~~\exists\rho\in FT^{*}(P).~\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.

Proof A.6
.

“
\Leftarrow
”: Let
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
.
With structural induction on the derivation of
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)

I show that, for any
\sigma
with
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
,
one has
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

If
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the first rule of Table
3
, then
\sigma=\rho=\top
and
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

Assume
\rho\mathbin{=}a\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the second rule.
Then
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
.
Moreover,
\sigma\mathbin{=}b\zeta
with
(a,b)\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}
and
\eta\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\zeta)
.
So
\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle b$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})
by Table
1
, and
\zeta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
by induction.
It follows that
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

Assume
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the third rule of Table
3
.
Then
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
.
So
\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})
by Table
1
, and
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
by induction.
It follows that
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

Assume
\rho\mathbin{=}Y\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the fourth rule.
Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}Y\cup\{\tau\}
and
\eta\in FT^{*}(P)
.
Moreover,
\sigma\mathbin{=}X\zeta
with
Y=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)
and
\eta\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\zeta)
.
So
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
,
and
\zeta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
by induction.
It follows that
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

Assume
\rho\mathbin{=}Y\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the fifth rule.
Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}Y\cup\{\tau\}
,

P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
\rho\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
.
Moreover,
\sigma\mathbin{=}X\zeta
with
Y=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)
.
So
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
,

\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})
and
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
by induction.
It follows that
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

Assume
\rho\mathbin{=}Ya\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
is obtained from the sixth rule.
Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}Y\cup\{\tau\}
,

P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
,
a\mathbin{\in}Y
and
a\eta\in FT^{*}(P)
.
Moreover,
\sigma\mathbin{=}Xb\zeta
with
Y\mathbin{=}\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)
,
(a,b)\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}
,

b\mathbin{\in}X
and
a\eta\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}^{-1}(b\zeta)
.
So
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
,

\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})
and
b\zeta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
by induction.
It follows that
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

“
\Rightarrow
”: Let
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.
With structural induction on the derivation of
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
I show that
there exists a
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
with
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.

If
\sigma=\top\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
is obtained from the first rule of Table
3
, take

\rho=\top
. Now
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
.

Assume
\sigma=b\zeta\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
is obtained from the second rule of Table
3
.
Then
\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle b$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})
and
\zeta\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
.
So
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
for some
a
with
(a,b)\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}
, and by induction there is an

\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
with
\eta\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\zeta)
.
Take
\rho=a\eta
. Then
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
and
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.

Assume
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
is obtained from the third rule of Table
3
.
Then
\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})
and
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
.
So
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
, and by induction there is a

\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
with
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.
Hence
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
.

Assume
\sigma=X\zeta\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
is obtained from the fourth rule of Table
3
.
Then
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
and
\zeta\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.
So
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)\cup\{\tau\}
.
By induction there is an
\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
with
\eta\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\zeta)
.
Take
\rho=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)\eta
. Then
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
by the fourth rule of Table
3
.
In case
\zeta
has the form
b\zeta^{\prime}
, then
\eta
has the form

a\eta^{\prime}
with
(a,b)\in\mathcal{R}
; moreover, yields
b\notin X
as well as
a\notin\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)
.
Consequently,
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.

Assume
\sigma=X\zeta\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
is obtained from the fifth rule of Table
3
.
Then
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
,
\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})

and
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
.
So
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)\cup\{\tau\}
,
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
,
and by induction there is a
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
with
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.
Of course
\rho
must have the form
\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)\eta
.
So
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
by the fifth rule of Table
3
.

Finally assume
\sigma=Xb\zeta\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
is obtained from the sixth rule of Table
3
.
Then
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
,
b\in X
,
\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})

and
b\zeta\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
.
So
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)\cup\{\tau\}
,
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
,
and by induction there is an
a\eta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
with
(a,b)\in\mathcal{R}
and
\eta\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\zeta)
.
So
a\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)
. Take
\rho=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)a\eta
. Then
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
by the sixth rule of Table
3
.
Moreover,
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.

Proposition A.7
.

spar explicit rooted

FT^{r*}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)=\textit{col}(FT^{r*}(P)\mathbin{\|_{S}}FT^{r*}(Q))
.

Proof A.8
.

“
\supseteq
”: Let
\sigma\in FT^{r*}(P)\mathbin{\|_{S}}FT^{r*}(Q)
, and fix a valid decomposition of

\sigma
as a witness.
With the clearly valid generalisation of Observation
5
to
FT^{r*}(P)
it suffices to show that
\sigma\in FT^{r*}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
.

•

Let
\sigma=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}
. Then
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(P)
and
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(Q)
.
So
{\it stable}(P)
and
{\it stable}(Q)
. Indeed, this implies
{\it stable}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
, and thus
\sigma\in FT^{r*}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
.

•

Let
\sigma\mathbin{=}\top
,
\sigma\mathbin{=}a\rho
or
\sigma\mathbin{=}X\rho
.
Then
\sigma_{\rm L}\in FT^{*}(P)
and
\sigma_{\rm R}\in FT^{*}(Q)
,
so from (the proof of) Proposition
one obtains
\sigma\in FT^{*}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)\subseteq FT^{r*}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
.

•

Let
\sigma={\rm t}X\rho
. Assume that
\sigma_{\rm L}\mathbin{=}{\rm t}X_{\rm L}\rho_{\rm L}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(P)
and

\sigma_{\rm R}\mathbin{=}X_{\rm R}\rho_{\rm R}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(Q)
; the other case follows by symmetry.
Then
\sigma_{\rm R}\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(Q)
. Moreover,
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(Q)
, so
Q
is stable.
By Definition
,
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
,
X_{\rm L}\rho_{\rm L}\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})

and
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for
\alpha\in X_{\rm L}\cup\{\tau\}
.
Thus
P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q
by Table
1
, and
X\rho\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime}\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
by
(the proof of) Proposition
.
Furthermore,
X_{\rm R}\rho_{\rm R}\in FT^{*}(Q)
must be derived by the fourth to sixth rule of Table
3
,
so
Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in X_{\rm R}\cup\{\tau\}
.
Hence
P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in X\cup\{\tau\}
, using (
7
).
Therefore,
\sigma\in FT^{r*}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
.

•

Let
\sigma\mathbin{=}\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}
. Then either
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(P)\wedge\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(Q)
, or

\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(P)\wedge\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(Q)
, or
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(P)\wedge\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(Q)
.
Either way,
P
and
Q
both have paths of
\tau
-transitions to a stable state, and at least one
of them is nonempty. It follows that
P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q
has a nonempty path of
\tau
-transitions to a
stable state, so
\sigma\in FT^{r*}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
.

“
\subseteq
”: Let
FT^{re}(P)
be defined exactly as
FT^{r*}(P)
—see Definition
—but writing

{\rm t}XX\sigma
instead of
{\rm t}X\sigma
, and employing

FT^{e}(P)
and
FT^{e}(P^{\prime})
, as defined in the proof of Proposition
, instead of
FT^{*}(P)
and
FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
.
Obviously,
FT^{r*}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)\subseteq\textit{col}(FT^{re}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q))
, so, using that
col
is monotonous, it
suffices to show that
FT^{re}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)\subseteq FT^{r*}(P)\mathbin{\|_{S}}FT^{r*}(Q)
.

So let
\sigma\in FT^{re}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
. With structural induction on the derivation of

\sigma\in FT^{re}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
from the (amended) rules of Tables
1
and
3

I show that
\sigma\in FT^{r*}(P)\mathbin{\|_{S}}FT^{r*}(Q)
. This means that in case
\sigma\mathbin{\neq}\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab},\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}

I have to give a valid decomposition of
\sigma
such that
\sigma_{\rm L}\in FT^{r*}(P)
and
\sigma_{\rm R}\in FT^{r*}(Q)
;
moreover, I have to show that
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(P)
and
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(Q)
in case
\sigma
has the form
{\rm t}X\rho
.

•

Let
\sigma\mathbin{=}\top
,
\sigma\mathbin{=}a\rho
or
\sigma\mathbin{=}X\rho
.
Then
\sigma\in FT^{e}(P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q)
. So by the proof of Proposition

there is a valid decomposition of
\sigma
such that
\sigma_{\rm L}\in FT^{*}(P)\subseteq FT^{r*}(P)

and
\sigma_{\rm R}\in FT^{*}(Q)\subseteq FT^{r*}(Q)
.

•

Let
\sigma=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}
.
Then
P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
, so
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
and
Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
.
So
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(P)
and
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(Q)
.

•

Let
\sigma={\rm t}XX\rho
. By Definition
,

P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in X\cup\{\tau\}
,

P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q^{\prime}
and
X\rho\in FT^{e}(P^{\prime}\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q^{\prime})
.
It follows that
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
and
Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
, so
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(P)
and
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(P)
.
By the proof of Proposition
there is a valid decomposition of
X\rho
such that

X_{\rm L}\rho_{\rm L}\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
and
X_{\rm R}\rho_{\rm R}\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(Q^{\prime})
.
Depending on which rule of Table
1
derived

P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q^{\prime}
,

(1)

either
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime}=Q
,

(2)

or
P^{\prime}=P
and
Q\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}Q^{\prime}
.

For reasons of symmetry, I may assume the former.

Let
X_{\rm L}^{\prime}:=\{a\in X_{\rm L}\mid P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}}\}
and

X_{\rm R}^{\prime}:=(X\setminus S)\cup\{a\in X\cap S\mid Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle a$\>}\hfil}}$}}}\}
.
Using the fourth rule of Table
3
,

X_{\rm L}^{\prime}X_{\rm L}\rho_{\rm L}\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
, so by Definition

\sigma_{\rm L}:={\rm t}X_{\rm L}^{\prime}X_{\rm L}\rho_{\rm L}\in FT^{r*}(P)
.
Moreover, by the fourth rule of Table
3
,

\sigma_{\rm R}:=X_{\rm R}^{\prime}X_{\rm R}\rho_{\rm R}\in FT^{*}(Q)\subseteq FT^{r*}(Q)
, using that
Q{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}

for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}(X{\setminus}S)\cup\{\tau\}
.
It remains to show the validity of the decomposition of
\sigma
into
\sigma_{\rm L}
and
\sigma_{\rm R}
.
This proceeds exactly as in the corresponding case in the proof of Proposition

(the case
\sigma=XX\rho
in direction “
\subseteq
”).

•

Let
\sigma=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}
. Then
P\mathbin{\|_{S}}Q
has a nonempty path of
\tau
-transitions to a stable state.
This path projects to paths of
\tau
-transitions from
P
and from
Q
to stable states, and at
least one of them must be nonempty. It follows that either
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(P)\wedge\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(Q)
, or

\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(P)\wedge\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(Q)
, or
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(P)\wedge\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(Q)
.

Proposition A.9
.

abstraction explicit rooted
Let
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\neq\sigma\neq\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}
.
Then

\sigma\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))~~\Leftrightarrow~~\exists\rho\in FT^{r*}(P).~\tau_{I}(\rho)=\sigma\cup I.

Moreover,
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))~~\Leftrightarrow~~\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(P)\wedge\mathcal{I}(P)\cap I=\emptyset
,

and
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))~~\Leftrightarrow~~\begin{array}[t]{@{}ll@{}}(\exists c_{0}c_{1}\dots c_{n}I\top\in FT^{r*}(P)\mbox{~where~}n\geq 0\mbox{~and all~}c_{i}\in I)\\
\mbox{}\vee(\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\in FT^{r*}(P)\wedge I\top\in FT^{r*}(P)).\end{array}

Proof A.10
.

Let
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\neq\sigma\neq\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}
. Since each state
R
reachable from
\tau_{I}(P)
satisfies
R{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in I
,
a trivial induction shows that

\sigma\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))\Leftrightarrow\sigma\cup I\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P)).

“
\Leftarrow
”: Let
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(P)
and
\rho
survives abstraction from
I
.
By Condition (i) in the definition of abstraction survival,

\tau_{I}(\rho)
is of the form
\sigma\cup I
. In view of (*), it suffices to show that

\tau_{I}(\rho)\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.

Suppose
\rho\mathbin{=}\top
,
\rho\mathbin{=}a\eta
or
\rho\mathbin{=}X\eta
.
Then
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)
. So
\tau_{I}(\rho)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))\subseteq FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))

by the proof of Proposition
.

Suppose
\rho\mathbin{=}{\rm t}X\eta
.
Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in X\cup\{\tau\}
,
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
X\eta\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
, by Definition
.
So
\tau_{I}(X\eta)\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}))
by the proof of Proposition
.
Moreover,
\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
and
\tau_{I}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all

\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
, using that
I\subseteq X
.
Note that
\tau_{I}(X\eta)
has the form
X\zeta
, although not necessarily with
\zeta=\tau_{I}(\eta)
.
Definition
yields
\tau_{I}(\rho)={\rm t}\tau_{I}(X\eta)\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.

“
\Rightarrow
”: Let
\sigma\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))
; by (*) I may assume that
\sigma=\sigma\cup I
.
I have to find a
\rho\in FT^{r*}(P)
such that
\tau_{I}(\rho)=\sigma
.

Suppose
\sigma\mathbin{=}\top
,
\sigma\mathbin{=}a\eta
or
\sigma\mathbin{=}X\eta
.
Then
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
. So by the proof of Proposition

there is a
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)\subseteq FT^{r*}(P)
such that
\tau_{I}(\rho)=\sigma
.

Suppose
\sigma\mathbin{=}{\rm t}X\zeta
. Then
\tau_{I}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in X\cup\{\tau\}
,

\tau_{I}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\tau_{I}(P^{\prime})
and
X\zeta\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}))
, by Definition
.
Hence
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in X\cup\{\tau\}
, and
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
.
By the proof of Proposition
there is a
X\eta\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
such that
\tau_{I}(X\eta)=X\zeta
.
So
\rho:={\rm t}X\eta\in FT^{r*}(P)
by Definition
, and
\tau_{I}(\rho)=\sigma
.

The second statement of Proposition
is trivial.
Now consider the third.

“
\Leftarrow
”: Suppose
\exists c_{0}c_{1}\dots c_{n}I\top\in FT^{r*}(P)\mbox{~where~}n\geq 0\mbox{~and all~}c_{i}\in I
.
Then
P
has a nonempty path, all of which transitions are labelled
\tau
or
c_{i}\in I
, ending in a
state
P^{\prime}
satisfying
P^{\prime}{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in I\cup\{\tau\}
.
Consequently,
\tau_{I}(P)
has a nonempty part, all of which transitions are labelled
\tau
, ending in
a state
\tau(P^{\prime})
satisfying
\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
. Consequently,
\tau_{I}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
and

\emptyset\top\in FT^{*}(\tau_{I}(P))
. Thus
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.

Now suppose
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\in FT^{r*}(P)\wedge I\top\in FT^{r*}(P)
.
Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
and
P
has a path, all of which transitions are labelled
\tau
, ending in
a state
P^{\prime}
satisfying
P^{\prime}{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in I\cup\{\tau\}
.
This path must be nonempty. Again it follows that
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))
.

“
\Leftarrow
”: Suppose
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\in FT^{r*}(\tau_{I}(P))
. Then
\tau_{I}(P)
has a nonempty part, all of
which transitions are labelled
\tau
, ending in a state
\tau(P^{\prime})
satisfying
\tau_{I}(P^{\prime}){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
.
Consequently,
P
has a nonempty path, all of which transitions are labelled
\tau
or
c\in I
, ending in a
state
P^{\prime}
satisfying
P^{\prime}{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\in I\cup\{\tau\}
.
In case on this path some transitions are labelled
c\mathbin{\in}I
, one obtains

\exists c_{0}c_{1}\dots c_{n}I\top\in FT^{r*}(P)\mbox{~where~}n\mathbin{\geq}0
and all
c_{i}\mathbin{\in}I
.
In case there are no such transitions,
\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\in FT^{r*}(P)
and
I\top\in FT^{r*}(P)
.

Proposition A.11
.

renaming rooted

\sigma\in FT^{r*}(\mathcal{R}(P))~~\Leftrightarrow~~\exists\rho\in FT^{r*}(P).~\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.

Proof A.12
.

“
\Leftarrow
”: Let
\rho\in FT^{r*}(P)
, and let
\sigma
satisfy
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.
I have to show that
\sigma\in FT^{r*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

Let
\rho\mathbin{=}\top
,
\rho\mathbin{=}a\eta
or
\rho\mathbin{=}X\eta
.
Then
\rho\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(P)
, so by Proposition

\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))\subseteq FT^{r*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

Let
\rho={\rm t}Y\eta
.
Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}Y\cup\{\tau\}
,

P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
and
Y\eta\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
.
Moreover,
\sigma\mathbin{=}{\rm t}X\zeta
with
Y\mathbin{=}\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)
and
Y\eta\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X\zeta)
.
Consequently
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
,

\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})
and
X\zeta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
by Proposition
.
It follows that
\sigma\mathbin{\in}FT^{r*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

Let
\rho=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}
. Then
{\it stable}(P)
, so
{\it stable}(\mathcal{R}(P))
and
\sigma=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

Let
\rho=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}
. Then
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
and
\emptyset\top\in FT^{*}(P)
.
So
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
and
\emptyset\top\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.
Therefore
\sigma=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\in FT^{r*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.

“
\Rightarrow
”: Let
\sigma\in FT^{r*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
. I have to find a
\rho\in FT^{r*}(P)
with
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.

Let
\sigma\mathbin{=}\top
,
\sigma\mathbin{=}a\eta
or
\sigma\mathbin{=}X\eta
.
Then
\sigma\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
, so by Proposition
there is a
\rho\in FT^{*}(P)\subseteq FT^{r*}(P)
with
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.

Let
\sigma={\rm t}X\zeta
. Then
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}X\cup\{\tau\}
,

\mathcal{R}(P)\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime})
and
X\zeta\mathbin{\in}FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P^{\prime}))
.
By Proposition
there is a
Y\eta\in FT^{*}(P^{\prime})
with
Y\eta\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X\zeta)
.
In particular
Y=\mathcal{R}^{-1}(X)
. Therefore,
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{$\mathord{-}\mkern-6.0mu\leaders{\hbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{-}\mkern-2.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-6.0mu\mathord{\not}\mkern-2.0mu\mathord{\rightarrow}$}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\alpha$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
for all
\alpha\mathbin{\in}Y\cup\{\tau\}
.
Moreover,
P\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$\>}\hfil}}$}}P^{\prime}
. Take
\rho:={\rm t}Y\eta
. Then
\rho\in FT^{r*}(P)
and
\rho\in\mathcal{R}^{-1}(\sigma)
.

Let
\sigma=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}
. Then
{\it stable}(\mathcal{R}(P))
, so
{\it stable}(P)
and
\rho=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc stab}\in FT^{r*}(P)
.

Let
\sigma=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}
. Then
\mathcal{R}(P){\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
and
\emptyset\top\in FT^{*}(\mathcal{R}(P))
.
So
P{\mathrel{\hbox{$\mathop{\hbox to15.33325pt{\rightarrowfill}}\limits^{\hbox to15.33325pt{\hfil\hbox{\vrule height=6.45831pt,depth=3.44444pt,width=0.0pt\>$\scriptstyle\tau$\>}\hfil}}$}}}
and
\emptyset\top\in FT^{*}(P)
. Therefore
\rho=\mbox{\scriptsize\sc poststab}\in FT^{r*}(P)
.
